18:["$","section",null,{"className":"drh-step","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"drh-prose","children":[["$","$L4c",null,{"as":"h2","id":"1-the-problem-who-frees-the-memory","children":"1. The problem: who frees the memory?"}],["$","p",null,{"children":"A program needs memory for things whose size is not known in advance: the text\nof a string, the items of a list. That memory has to be handed back when the\nprogram is done with it, or the computer runs out. Someone has to decide\nwhen \"done\" is. Languages answer this in three ways."}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"You do it by hand (C)."}]," You ask for memory with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"malloc"}]," and give it back with\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"free"}],". Nothing checks that you did it right, and three mistakes are easy to make:"]}],["$","p",null,{"children":["Leave out the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"free"}]," and you have a ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"leak"}],": the memory is never returned, and\na long-running program slowly eats the machine. Use after free and double free\nare worse. They corrupt memory, and the program may crash far from the bug, or\ncarry on and do something an attacker chose. A large share of security holes in\nC and C++ programs are exactly these mistakes."]}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"A garbage collector does it for you (Python, JavaScript, Java)."}]," The runtime\ntracks which values can still be reached and frees the rest. This is safe and\neasy, and the price is paid while the program runs: the collector needs CPU time\nand extra memory, it may pause your program at moments you do not choose, and\nyou cannot say exactly when a value is destroyed (which makes it awkward to\nclose a file or release a lock \"right when I am done\")."]}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"The compiler does it for you (Drachm)."}]," Drachm has no garbage collector and no\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"free"}],". Instead the compiler follows two ideas:"]}],["$","ol",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Every value has exactly ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"one owner"}],", and the value is destroyed when its\nowner goes out of scope."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Anything else that wants to use the value must ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"borrow"}]," it, and the compiler\nchecks that no borrow can outlive the value or get in the way of a change."]}],"\n"]}],["$","p",null,{"children":["The compiler proves this by reading your program, before it runs. The finished\nprogram contains the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"free"}]," calls in the right places, exactly as if you had\nwritten them correctly in C, and nothing is checked while it runs. Double free\nand use after free cannot be written in ordinary code, and an owned value is never\nforgotten. A program that tries is rejected with an error that points at the line."]}],["$","p",null,{"children":"That is the whole trade. You learn a few rules and the compiler sometimes says\nno; in return you get C's speed with a garbage collector's safety. The rest of\nthis guide is those rules, one idea at a time."}]]}],["$","div",null,{"className":"drh-code-col","children":["$","$L4d",null,{"className":"shiki shiki-themes vitesse-light vitesse-dark","style":{"--shiki-light":"#393a34","--shiki-dark":"#dbd7caee","--shiki-light-bg":"#ffffff","--shiki-dark-bg":"#121212"},"tabIndex":"0","icon":"<svg viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path d=\"M16.5921 9.1962s-.354-3.298-3.627-3.39c-3.2741-.09-4.9552 2.474-4.9552 6.14 0 3.6651 1.858 6.5972 5.0451 6.5972 3.184 0 3.5381-3.665 3.5381-3.665l6.1041.365s.36 3.31-2.196 5.836c-2.552 2.5241-5.6901 2.9371-7.8762 2.9201-2.19-.017-5.2261.034-8.1602-2.97-2.938-3.0101-3.436-5.9302-3.436-8.8002 0-2.8701.556-6.6702 4.047-9.5502C7.444.72 9.849 0 12.254 0c10.0422 0 10.7172 9.2602 10.7172 9.2602z\" fill=\"currentColor\" /></svg>","children":"$L4e"}]}]]}]
